# English Lab project guidance

**Read [docs/REQUIREMENTS.md](docs/REQUIREMENTS.md) first: "How we work - Work requirements", the one rulebook** (Roger, 20 Nov 2026; `CLAUDE.md` loads it): every rule for working with Roger in one organised document, unifying the Drive guides of Teacher Studio › 01_How we work and 08_Reference docs (exams, rubrics, Drive rules, prompts, programmes, competences) with the standing rules (workflow, privacy, what students see, curriculum, LS design, exams, rubrics, marking, answering students, emails, deploying), and the outdated parts and contradictions it settled. **Where an older line in this file disagrees with a newer one or with REQUIREMENTS.md, Roger's newest decision is the valid one** (Roger, 21 Nov 2026); lines marked *Replaced* are kept only as history. This file stays the technical reference: when a task sets a new standing rule, add it there and its technical detail here, in the same PR.

All docs are mapped in [docs/README.md](docs/README.md), which is also Roger's **teaching materials index** (Roger, 26 Oct 2026): one table per course with a row per LS linking programme section, design, rubric, grammar and vocabulary, and practice exam. When a task adds an LS docs folder, a rubric, a study PDF or a practice exam, update its row in the same PR. Real exams and answer keys stay in Roger's Google Drive, never in this public repo; the index only links the Drive folders. **Drive layout (Roger, 26 Oct 2026):** My Drive > Feina > Teacher Studio, with `00 Start here` (a Doc linking every folder), `01_General` (official documents, competences, levels, grammar and lexicon by level, how we work incl. exam and Forms prompts, calendar, `07_Word lists (Lexora)`, `08_Reference docs`), `02_Cursos` > `2 ESO` and `4 ESO` (each `Programme` and `LS1 …` to `LS6 …`, an LS holding `Exam` with `Google Forms script`, `Summaries`, `Final product`, `Assessment`, `Listenings` and `Videos` as needed), `03_Site assets`, `04_Students (private)`, `05_Archive` (top-level names set by Roger). Each programme folder holds one current file, the version with the Beginners route (it contains every route); older versions are in its `Archive`. **Drive rules (Roger, 26 Oct 2026; Doc in `01_General/How we work`):** every new document Claude makes (exams, keys, exam audios, Forms scripts, programmes, rubrics, summaries, assessment, competences, levels) is saved in its Drive folder as soon as it is done; every LS listening (the mp3s under `<ls>/audio/`, normal and slow) is also copied to that LS's `Listenings` folder, and **every new audio Claude creates goes there as soon as it is made** (Roger, 26 Oct 2026; exam audio goes to the LS's `Exam` folder), every LS video (the mp4s under `<ls>/video/`, normal and slow; no subtitles or posters) is copied to that LS's `Videos` folder in the same way (Roger, 28 Oct 2026), while other site media stay only in the repo; design and assessment docs from `docs/<course>/ls<n>-*/` (criteria, competences grid, indicators, grading, final product) and the site rubric (`final-rubric-data.js`, all routes) go as Google Docs to the LS's `Assessment` and `Final product` folders, marked as copies of the repo version, which stays the master; the reference docs in `docs/general/` that say how things are made (the new LS playbook, the research audit brief, the class games guide) go as Google Docs to `01_General/08_Reference docs`, marked as copies, and are replaced there (old copy to its `Archive`) in the same task whenever the repo version changes; a changed document is updated in place; a document that gets old moves to the `Archive` subfolder of the same folder (date in the name if a newer file has the same name); never delete anything in Roger's Drive. Binary files (PDF, mp3, Word) reach Drive through Roger's PC (Google Drive for desktop, `G:\Mi unidad\Feina\Teacher Studio`); Google Docs are created directly. Planned clean-up of the code structure: [roadmap](docs/general/roadmap.md). Pending site tasks: [tasks](docs/general/tasks.md). **Building a new LS:** follow the [new LS playbook](docs/general/new-ls-playbook.md). **Research audit (Roger, 26 Oct 2026):** the approved changes are in [REQUIREMENTS.md](docs/REQUIREMENTS.md) › 5 (the old brief is archived in [docs/archive/general/](docs/archive/general/research-audit-brief.md)). 4ESO LS2 is the model (Roger, 25 Oct 2026): a new LS copies LS2 as it is now, and any change Roger makes to LS2 also updates the playbook in the same PR, unless he says it is for one LS only.

## How it works and Help (24 Nov 2026)
- Teacher navigation uses the distinct `how` tab in `teacher.html` (desktop and mobile); `matTab` contains only teaching materials. The correction presentation lives at `shared/help/how-i-correct-your-work.html` and is linked from the teacher guide and the student `today.html#help` guide only. It is a self-contained slide player with the original slide artwork, text transcripts, previous/next, keyboard and touch navigation, and full screen. The old PDF remains unlinked for backward compatibility. No database writes or announcements. See `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`.

## Shared source of truth
- This file is shared by Roger's two Claude projects: the LS project (Learning Situations, content design) and the English Lab project (site code and fixes). Before working on any Learning Situation, `git pull` and read it.
- When a task makes a technical decision (page structure, file locations, levels per route, how activities, progress or submission work, audio and video rules), write it here in the right section and commit and push it in the same task. Keep entries short and concrete, and update or remove outdated ones instead of adding duplicates.
- Docs are organised by course, then by LS: `docs/<course>/programme.md` (Roger's teaching programme) and `docs/<course>/ls<n>-<name>/` with `AGENTS.md` (rules for that LS plus a dated decision log) and `overview.md` (the approved content). Generic docs are in `docs/general/`. They live in the repo, not only in a project's shared folder, so both projects can read them.
- Decisions that apply to every LS go in this file; decisions for one LS go in that LS's `AGENTS.md`.

## Start from working examples
- Before implementing a page or feature, inspect the closest successful page in the repository and reuse its structure, styles and interaction patterns where appropriate.
- **LS names carry their number (Roger, 7 Nov 2026: "we should see LS2 Free Time..."):** wherever students see an LS it is "LS2 · Free Time, Live!": `elLsName(l)` in `el-catalog.js` (number = place in its course in `EL_LS`) for the board and the LS home; in the side menus the number replaces 📚 in the icon box (`elLsNum`, `.ic.lsn`, Roger 7 Nov 2026: "instead of the book emoji") and the label is the name alone; the LS's own pages (from LS2 on) write it in `<title>`, `h1`, the ‹ back link and the brand line. LS1's own pages are left as they are.
- **LS landing page (`<ls>/index.html`), LS2 on (Roger, 6 Nov 2026: "Redesign the LS so that they fit the new students and teacher design and students go from the dashboard to the ls and the ls to the dashboard"):** copy `4eso/seasons-and-celebrations/index.html`, in the board's style: `body.lsh data-ls="<ls id>"`, `shared/css/ls-home.css` (course colours `--c`, `--c-dark`, `--c-soft` from the LP theme: 2 ESO green, 4 ESO blue) and `shared/js/el-ls-home.js` after `el-catalog.js`, `el-plan.js`, `el-week.js`. The page holds the content; the script adds the board's side menu (student: 🏠 My activities, this LS, All Learning Situations, English Lab, Practise, Help; teacher: 📊 Dashboard and every LS of both courses from `EL_LS`; a phone gets the board's bottom bar), a ‹ My activities / ‹ Dashboard link at the top, the **Continue** card (the first open, not dropped learning path not done in the group's time plan order, or the checkpoint it needs first) and each row's progress in `ol.lps` (`a.lp[data-a="activity id"]`: ✓ done, steps done, 🔒 checkpoint first, Next). **Students see only the learning paths the teacher has opened for their group** (Roger, 6 Nov 2026: "I don't want students to see the future LP as depending on the time left they might not do them"): not open yet and dropped ones are left out (with none open: "Your teacher will open the learning paths in class"); the teacher and testers see all. No new Firestore reads: it uses the progress `el-cloud.js` copied into the browser, `ELCloud.lsNow` (the cached `ls/{id}`) and the cached results. Home = head, Continue, learning paths, the challenge card and tiles (no 📚 Study place since 7 Nov 2026, Roger: "the study section makes no sense, delete it": the summary PDF is on the board's 🔤 Grammar and vocabulary place, the reading book on 📖 Books; the page keeps the grammar and vocabulary lists in a hidden `<div data-ls-lists>` with two `.bigcard` lists, which that place reads, so keep it in a new LS page; LS1 too · 📈 My formative assessment · 🗣 Shadowing video · ⓘ About this LS `#about` as visual cards · 🖨 Paper worksheets for the teacher; no 🎮 Vocabulary games tile; the 🧩📚 Games and theory card sits beside the LS title. **Every new LS: tick the site look checklist in `docs/general/new-ls-playbook.md` Part 1b** (Roger, 7 Nov 2026: "I need them to be remembered for the next LS")); `#final` holds the final product card. UI labels follow the student's level (`data-ca` holds the Catalan text for Starter and Base). The board home shows the current LS itself under Continue and the tiles (Roger, 7 Nov 2026: "students should see in their dashboard the current LS"; `lsNowHtml` in `today.html`): it reads this page (`ol.lps`, `a.chal`, `.tiles`, `#shadowDlg video`; one static fetch, kept as `localStorage.el_lsh_<id>`), so keep those class names when you build a new LS page; progress, opened-only rows and Next come from what the board already read (no new Firestore reads). Preview without Firebase: `window.EL_DEMO_LS` (see the file). **LS1 too (Roger, 6 Nov 2026: "yes LS1 new design"):** both `my-real-routine/index.html` pages use the same design with their content unchanged (activity ids without `ls2-`, no Study place, a 📝 Sample exam tile instead of the shadowing video, no reading book); the rest of LS1 stays as it is. **One final product page (Roger, 25 Oct 2026):** the Final Product tab is only a short card (challenge, one or two sentences, an "Open the final product →" button to the final product Learning Path); the route requirements, "What counts in the final assessment?", the rubric and the steps live only on the final product page. That page opens with "See a model final product" (the model for the student's route, where the LS has one), then the task for the route and what counts, then the steps.
- **Model final product = a video with sound (Roger, 14 Nov 2026: "examples with audio of the LS2 final tasks"):** every `<ls>/<route>-model.html` (LS2 on) shows its model as a video with sound and captions above the slides (🎬 Watch / 🖼 Slides one by one, `shared/js/el-model-video.js`, Catalan buttons with `data-ca="1"` on Base pages). Make it free with `tools/model-video` (`voice.py`: Kokoro voices per speaker from `voices.json`, recorded sentence by sentence; `build.js`: slide screenshots + ffmpeg -> `<ls>/video/<route>-model.mp4`, `.vtt`, `-poster.jpg`); rerun both whenever a model script or slide changes and bump `data-v`. Copy the mp4s to the LS's Drive `Videos` folder.
- **Publishing a new LS:** add its card to the Learning Situations grid in the home `index.html` and in `<course>/index.html`, and its `↳ LS<n> · <title>` link (numbered within the course, Roger 26 Oct 2026). Its place among the course's entries in `EL_LS` (`el-catalog.js`) is its LS number: the teacher dashboard's LS dropdown shows `LS<n> · <name>` from that order (Roger, 30 Oct 2026) to the Learning Situations dropdown of every page that has one (`2eso/index.html`, `4eso/index.html` and each LS `index.html`). Links in those dropdowns are relative to the page they sit on. Every page keeps the full dropdown in its HTML; for a student (and the tester in a group's view) `lsMenu` in `el-cloud.js` removes the other courses, the "Soon" group and the LSs of their course that are hidden or not assigned to their group (Roger, 26 Oct 2026), so a new LS needs no extra code there; the teacher and the tester's full view see everything.
- Use each course’s `my-real-routine/getting-started.html` as the shell/topbar reference. Reuse `shared/css/layout.css`, `topbar.css`, `activities.css` and existing shared helpers rather than creating a new visual system.
- **Learning Path top bar (Roger, 25 Oct 2026):** the path appears once, in the "Learning Path" selector (students use it to go to another Learning Path). On pages with that selector, `lp-theme-2eso.css`/`lp-theme-4eso.css` hide the brand number and title; keep the markup, and keep the selector's options complete and ordered (`section-next.js` reads them for "continue to the next Learning Path").
- **Class presentations (grammar and vocabulary decks):** one self-contained page per deck in `lab/grammar/<course>-<topic>.html` (grammar) or `lab/vocabulary/<course>-<topic>.html` (vocabulary), in the course palette, listed in the English Lab Grammar or Vocabulary menu (`lab/index.html`) and linked from its Learning Path page with an `a.deck-link` and `?from=<that page>` (the ← button goes back there). **Vocabulary introductions (Roger, 25 Oct 2026):** the class guesses each new word first (a clue, a picture emoji or a sentence), then the reveal shows the word, a 🔊 button that says it with the browser's English voice (`speechSynthesis`, en-GB; on the projector or on each student's Chromebook) and its Catalan meaning, with a short reason; each section ends with a word wall or the pattern (sun → sunny). **New vocabulary introductions are class games, not slides (Roger, 30 Oct 2026):** the Autumn words slides of Getting Started (4 ESO Seasons & Celebrations) became the game `lab/game/decks/4eso-autumn-words.js` and the slide page and the English Lab Vocabulary menu were removed; see "Class games". **Question-first (Roger, 25 Oct 2026):** lecture decks made him talk too much, so new decks ask the class before they explain: every question shows how students answer (✋ 1–3 fingers, 👍/👎, ✏️ write it, 💬 turn and talk, 🎲 random class-list number, 🏆 team points) and → / Space / a presenter clicker reveals one step at a time (answer, then rule). Reference: `lab/grammar/4eso-present-simple-customs.html` (Roger, 30 Oct 2026: no longer linked from the Learning Path, the LP2 class game replaces it in class; it stays in the English Lab Grammar menu) (`data-step` shows an element, `data-open` reveals its answer, `data-until` removes it, `data-label` names the step on the Next button, `data-ca` holds the Catalan help). Never put student names in a deck: the picker uses class-list numbers. **Every presentation explains the team points (Roger, 25 Oct 2026):** right after the cover it has `<section class="slide" data-el-points></section>` (the reminder: the rules and a quick check, filled by `shared/js/el-deck-points.js`), the 🏆 Teams strip (`#teambar` with `data-course`, `#teamsBtn`) and, before the deck's own script, `shared/js/el-sound.js`, `shared/js/el-points.js` and `shared/js/el-deck-points.js`. **Sound effects (Roger, 25 Oct 2026):** `shared/js/el-sound.js` makes them with the Web Audio API (no sound files): `ELSound.play(name)` for the 3 · 2 · 1 countdown (`tick`, `go`), the random number (`pick`), the timer's end (`time`), + on the team strip (`point`) and the buzzer (`open`, `first`, `right`, `wrong` on the projector; `press`, `win` on students' devices); the deck's 🔊 tool or M mutes them in that browser (`el_sound`). See "Team points" under the teacher dashboard. **Every deck engine keeps the live hooks** (copy them from the reference deck): after each step it dispatches `deckstep` with `info()`; it exposes `window.ELDeck = {info, show, setCa}`; options carry `data-i` (their place before shuffling) and `info().order` sends each slide's order as a string ("2,1,0": Firestore refuses nested arrays); and while `live` is set (`.deck-live` on the root) it hides the nav buttons, tools and rail and ignores the student's keys except C (Catalan) and F (full screen). `#teambar` also has `data-ls` (the LS whose route decides Catalan). See "Buzzer and live view".
- **Class games (Roger, 26 Oct 2026: "like a Kahoot or a Blooket"):** one engine in `lab/game/`, and a game is only content. A new game = a deck file `lab/game/decks/<course>-<topic>.js` that calls `ELGameDeck({title, slides})` plus one line in `lab/game/decks/index.js` (`{id, title, course, emoji, ls:"LS2 · …", lp:"LP1 · …"}`; the board lists the games by course, then LS, then learning path: Roger, 30 Oct 2026). Slides, in order: theory `{theory, img, lines:[…]}` (**never shown in the game since 16 Nov 2026**, Roger: "during the game of vocab and grammar, I didn't want theory anymore": the board's Next jumps over every theory and 📝 notes slide, so a class game is questions only; the theory stays in the deck because it splits the questions into parts for 🎯 What to study, feeds `theory.js` / 📝 Your notes on the LS page and the Code Breakers help after a wrong answer). **A class game clears `live/{group}.mode` and `.cb`** (16 Nov 2026: a Code Breakers round had left `mode:"cb"`, so students got Code Breakers while the board showed the class game) and question `{q, img, options:[2–4], why}` where **the first option is the right one** (the board shuffles them, the same order on every reload; `keep:true` keeps the order, e.g. True / False). **Written questions (Roger, 30 Oct 2026):** `{q, img, write:['is cheering', "'s cheering"], why}`: students type the missing words on their device instead of tapping a tile and send them once (`a:{slide: text}` in the same answers doc, no rules change); `write` lists every accepted answer, the first is shown. The board checks them with `ELGame.norm` (lower case, straight apostrophes, single spaces, no final full stop) when it shows the answer, sends the normalised list as `keys[slide]`, and shows the wrong answers without names. The player gets only `q:{text, img, write:true}` before that. A game may alternate tap and written questions (`lab/game/decks/2eso-present-continuous.js`). Text: `**word**` is highlighted, `___` is a gap; `img` is an emoji or an image file. No timer: only right or wrong. **Every game follows these rules (Roger, 26 Oct 2026):** it is at **Safe** level (not easy), and it uses the learning paths' vocabulary in two ways: **passive** (the words appear in the questions and theory texts) and **active** (questions that ask for a word: meaning, use, collocation or preposition). Choose mainly the words that are not common at their level: the [S] lists of the LS overview (`docs/<course>/ls…/overview.md`), not the basic [I] ones. The teacher opens the board (`lab/game/host.html`, also linked from the dashboard's Points box), chooses the game and the group; from then on `host.js` publishes it in `live/{group}` (`game:true`, phase lobby | theory | q | reveal | end, the question and its options but never the right one before it is shown) and every signed-in student of that group is taken from any page into the player (`lab/game/play.html?g=…&from=…`: `el-cloud.js` and `el-deck-points.js` watch `live/{group}`). The player loads no deck file: it shows only the question and four coloured tiles (red ▲, blue ◆, gold ●, green ■), one tap, then green ✓ or red ✗. Answers go to `live/{group}/answers/{email}` and right answers become the same term points as the presentations (`points/{email}`, `teams/{group}.best`; bonus up to +0.5 in proportion to the group's best, `el-points.js`; the dashboard's Points box exports them as CSV). The answer shows by itself once everyone has answered. Music (`ELGame.music`, Web Audio, no files) plays on the board only; students' devices play only their own tap and result sounds. **Two games per learning path (Roger, 5 Nov 2026: "each Learning path should have a vocabulary only game and grammar only game. The grammar should have slides to explain it well so that students understand it"):** `<course>-ls<n>-lp<n>-vocab` (only vocabulary: meaning, use, collocation, preposition; no verb-form questions) and `<course>-ls<n>-lp<n>-grammar` (only the path's grammar; its words only in the texts), `kind:"vocab"|"grammar"` in `index.js`, titles `Vocabulary · …` / `Grammar · …`, both linked from the path's page (📚 Vocabulary game, 🧩 Grammar game). Done for LS2 LP1–LP3 of both courses (LP3 on 6 Nov 2026: "I need new games for LP3"; the older mixed LP3 decks were removed); LP4 on waits for Roger's ask. **Always a pair (Roger, 6 Nov 2026: "always games should be grammar and vocabulary"):** every new class game, in any LS and course (LS3 on too), is made as this vocabulary + grammar pair; never a mixed game again (*replaced 19 Nov 2026: decks stay a pair, but the board can play both together*). (The decks stay a pair; since 19 Nov 2026 the board can PLAY them together: 📚🧩 Vocabulary + Grammar · LSn on `host.html?ls=&kind=mix` ticks both kinds' points and saves each question to its own deck's point, see the guide item 18.) The grammar game opens each grammar point with explanation slides (form, use, examples and the typical mistake) and then 4–6 questions on it: a theory slide's `lines` can be text, a table `{table:[[header…],[row…]…]}` or a warning `{watch:"…"}`; `step:true` shows one block per Space (the rest keeps its place); `wide:true` for a big table; `ca:[…]` is Catalan help, hidden until the teacher presses CA (or C) on the board (kept in that browser, `el_game_ca`); `~~word~~` crosses out a mistake. Reference decks: `lab/game/decks/2eso-ls2-lp2-grammar.js`, `lab/game/decks/4eso-ls2-lp1-grammar.js`, `lab/game/decks/2eso-ls2-lp1-vocab.js` (tap and written questions alternate). **Results (Roger, 5 Nov 2026: "I need to see their results in a chart as group and individually"):** when a game ends (last slide, ■ End or Finish) the board writes one document `gameResults/{group}_{sid}` (teacher only in `firestore.rules`): the game, `kind`, the shown questions with how many got each right, and per student `{e, r, a, miss}`; `lab/game/results.html` (teacher only, linked from the board, the end screen, the dashboard's Points box and Materials) reads one group's documents plus its student list (one read per game played + one per student) and draws the group game by game (bars by kind, click a bar for its questions), a table per student (all, vocabulary, grammar, played; red under 50 %) and each student's games against the class with the questions to review. Testers are left out (student list only); `?demo` shows made-up sample data. **📝 Take your notes (Roger, 6 Nov 2026: "in the slides I explain theory, I want students to have a slide with: Take your notes: and then they can see on their LS: your notes"):** the board adds a notes slide by itself after each run of theory slides (`withNotes` in `host.js`; none with `notes:false` on the deck or on a theory slide; Next / Space skips it): the projector shows the theory of the run again in small and how many are writing; each student types notes on their device (`play.js`, phase `notes`, `live/{group}.nt`), saved in `students/{uid}/notes/{deck}` = {ls, course, deck, title, lp, parts:{p<k>:{topic, text, at}}, updated} (firestore.rules: their own, the teacher reads) 2.5 s after they stop typing; the same game again brings back what they wrote. The LS home page shows them in the place `#notes` (📝 Your notes tile, `shared/js/el-notes.js`, one query when it opens), **read only** and printable (Roger, 6 Nov 2026: "they can't edit, they only can see them after they do them in the formative games"); the teacher's student view shows them too. **The teacher sees each student's answers, performance and notes together, per LS** (Roger, 6 Nov 2026): the notes text also goes to the game's answers doc (`a:{slide: text}`), and `gameResults` keeps per student `ans:{n: what they tapped or wrote}` and `notes:{p<k>: text}`, plus `qs[].ok` and `nts:[{k, topic}]`; `results.html` has LS chips and, under each student, every game with a question table (their answer, ✓/✗, right answer) and their 📝 notes (no extra reads). **Theory, notes, results and what to study together (Roger, 6 Nov 2026: "they get a copy of the slides and below their notes … if a student doesn't come … the system saves the theory in their notes but without their notes"; "I want students to see their results in the games and suggestions of what to study"; "a circle with a percentage … two, grammar and vocabulary … next to the students in the list"):** `lab/game/decks/theory.js` holds only the theory slides of every deck grouped like the notes slides (**rebuild it with `node tools/game-theory/build.mjs` whenever a deck changes**); the player shows that theory next to the notes box. **Every part names its point (Roger, 9 Nov 2026: "shouldnt we put the kind of grammar and performace like present simple, and then a button to spot the mistakes and correct stuff?"):** the first theory slide of each part has `point:"…"` and `pointCa:"…"` (a short name students recognise, e.g. `Present Simple: he / she / it + -s`; give every new deck these); theory.js keeps them as `parts[].name` / `nameCa` with `n` (how many questions practise the part, never the questions), and the board saves `pn` (shown questions per part) in each `myGames` result. The student board's 🎓 My progress › 📚 Grammar and vocabulary (`gvBox` in today.html) shows each game with its points under it (% right, from `miss[].p` and `pn`) and **✏️ Fix my mistakes** per point (`fixPanel`): the student's wrong answers one by one (gap: write the right words; "Which is correct?": choose between their answer and the right one, or, saved without their answer, among all the options of the deck file `lab/game/decks/<deck>.js`, loaded then (`fxDeck`; not found: the right answer is shown); `.fxopt` sets its own dark text, as the page's buttons have white text (problem report, 4ESOA, 16 Nov 2026); the rule opens after a wrong try, the answer after two); fixed ones are kept on that device only (`el_gvfix`), nothing is written online and nothing counts for the marks. The board writes `ls/{id}.played[group][deck]` (once per game, on the first notes slide or the end) (games played before 6 Nov 2026 evening had none: `node tools/game-theory/backfill-played.mjs [--add GROUP:deck@date] [--apply]` copies them from `gameResults`, dry run by default). **Which group played which game** (Roger, 8 Nov 2026: "to make sure they all do them"): the teacher's view of the LS page's 📝 place is a table (games of the LS × groups of the course, ✓ day or Not yet, ▶ Play), and the board's game list and group buttons show ✓/✗ per group (one read per LS with games) and, at the end, `myGames/{email}.games[{group}_{sid}]` = {deck, title, kind, ls, lp, day, sid, r, of, miss:[{n, q, a, ok, p}]} (one write per player; rules: the student gets their own, the teacher writes). The LS page's 📝 place shows every game the group played: its result, 🎯 What to study (the theory parts with mistakes, then the questions with their answer and the right one), each theory part with the student's notes below it; a student who missed the game gets the theory with "No notes". Student board: a "Games and theory" link with two circles (🧩 grammar, 📚 vocabulary % right in this LS, `elRing`/`elGameStats` in `el-catalog.js`, one read of myGames per board load) to that place. Dashboard: the same two circles next to each student in the learning paths table (gameResults of the group, one read per game played, link to Results). Not on LS1. **Theory slides go between the questions** (about 3 theory slides and 15–20 questions in a vocabulary game, 3–4 explanation slides and about 16 questions in a grammar game), each followed by questions that practise it. Roger's readable guide: `docs/general/class-games-guide.md` (keep it in step with these rules, add each new game to its table, and update the copy in his Drive, Feina > Teacher Studio). **Each game is linked from its learning path for the teacher only** (Roger, 26 Oct 2026): on the page of the learning path whose content it closes, a `<p data-el-teacher hidden>` link to `lab/game/host.html?deck=<id>&from=/English-lab/<that page's path>` (el-cloud.js shows `[data-el-teacher]` only to the teacher's account); it opens the board on that game, ready to choose the group. **Back to the Learning Path (Roger, 30 Oct 2026):** the board shows "← Learning Path" (group choice, game list and end screen) to the `from` page, or to the page that linked to it if there is no `from` (never the dashboard, which has its own button); kept in the tab's sessionStorage (`elGameBack`). The game uses the live and answers documents of the class quiz. Testers answer and get points too (to try it), but their points never set the group's best: the best is taken over the student list only. **🕵️ Code Breakers (Roger, 11–12 Nov 2026: "can we make a game like a blooket, hackers like?"; our own name and look, never a copy of another game's names or art):** a second mode of every learning path, `lab/game/codebreakers.html` + `cb-host.js` (board), `cb-play.js` (the player: `play.js` hands over when `live/{group}.mode` is `"cb"`), `cb.js` (shared rules, `window.ELCB`), `cb.css`. A round = the questions of **both** games of the path (Roger: "if I choose Code Breakers, it picks the questions of the grammar and the vocabulary of that LP"; the one place where they mix), no theory, each student at their own pace, a timer the teacher chooses (5/8/10/15 min, +2 min, ■ End). **New questions per path (Roger, 12 Nov 2026: "different questions asking the same grammar and vocabulary … to make the games more complete"):** `lab/game/decks/more/<deck id>.js` = `ELGameMore({deck, questions:[…]})`, each question as in a deck plus `p` (the theory part it practises, 1 = the first run of theory slides); Code Breakers shows them first, then the class game's own; the class games never use them. Done: 2 ESO LP3; write them for every new pair of games. Password words come by themselves from the vocabulary questions with a gap and a one-word answer. In this mode the player loads the deck files (each student goes at their own pace) and the board recomputes every result from the raw answers; the board alone reads the passwords and judges the hacks (`live/{group}.cb.hr`), publishing at most every 4 s. The answers doc gets a `cb` map (firestore.rules). Results: two `gameResults` docs per round (`{group}_{sid}` vocabulary, `{group}_{sid+1}` grammar) with `mode:"cb"`, `cb:true`, and `myGames` entries with `of` = what that student answered; readers use the student's own `a` as the denominator when `x.cb` (results.html `den`, teacher.html `gameMine`/`stuGames`; a round counts from 3 answers) and `elLatestGames` keeps Code Breakers rounds apart from the classic play of the same deck. **🧩 Which grammar? (Roger, 16 Nov 2026: "When doing grammar games I want to choose the topics done in all the LS like: comparatives, ed/ing relative clauses… so we add more and more difficulty as we progress and if I don't have time to do whatever there are no questions about it"):** a grammar game (Quiz or Code Breakers) asks, after the group, which grammar points go in (`drawPoints` in `host.js`): every point of every grammar deck of the course (`kind:"grammar"` in `index.js`), by LS and learning path. A point = a run of theory slides (the 📝 notes part `p<k>`, named by its `point`); **give every point of a new grammar deck `tab:"<the LP page's grammar tab id>"`** and the deck's index line `act:"<course>/<LP page>"`, so it is ticked by itself when `ls/{id}.done[group]["<act>#<tab>"]` has it (Time plan; read with `played`, no extra reads); a point with no tab (Getting started games) is ticked once the group played that game; earlier LSs start unticked; this game's points all ticked if none would be. This game's ticked points keep all their questions, unticked ones none; each ticked point of another deck adds 1–3 (`el_game_rvn`, default 1) 🔁 review questions from that deck and its `more` file (seeded by `sid`), spread among the game's (`build()`; slides get `_p`, review ones `_rv`). Kept in the tab's `elGame` (`tp`, `rvn`), so a reload rebuilds the same game. Results stay this game's; review questions carry `d` (their deck) in `gameResults.qs`, and in `myGames` misses `d` + `dp` (their point, `p:""`) plus `px:{deck:{p<k>: shown}}`; `today.html` `gvBox` adds them to that deck's points (and ✏️ Fix my mistakes), `el-notes.js` lists them as "🔁 Review of earlier grammar"; `gameResults.tp` keeps the choice. **💬 Expressions in the grammar game (Roger, 16 Nov 2026: "include them in the grammar games"):** a 📚 vocabulary deck's point whose first theory slide has `ex:true` (play / go / do, how often, on / at / in + time, verb + preposition, both… and / unlike: 2 ESO LS2 LP1 ×2, LP3 ×1; 4 ESO LS2 LP2 ×1, LP3 ×2) is never in the vocabulary game; the 🧩 grammar game lists it under its learning path as "💬 Expressions · …" (`exDeck`, `ptsOf` in `host.js`), ticked like any point (its own deck's `act` + `tab`, or that vocabulary game played): `tab:"vocabEx"` (the Time plan's 💬 Expressions item; `doneDay` in `host.js` falls back to the Vocabulary tab when the group's agenda doesn't have the item), 4 ESO LP3 `grammarPrep` (Origins) and `grammarComp` (Comparing). It stays in its deck (the same `p<k>`, so notes and earlier results keep their place) and comes in like a review point (`_rv` with `d`, `dp`, `ex`): all its questions when it is the learning path the game is saved as (`own`, no 🔁 on the board, "💬 Expressions" instead), else 1, 2, 3 or all like any review; Code Breakers does the same (`cb.js` `sameLp`). A new expression point in a vocabulary deck = add `ex:true` to its theory slide. Code Breakers gets it as `codebreakers.html?…&tp=deck.1.2,deck2.3&rn=<1–3>&rs=<seed>` → `live/{group}.cb.{rv, tp, rn, rs}`; `ELCB.load(ids, wordIds, {rv, tp, n, seed})` filters the same on the board and every Chromebook (review keys `r<j>_<n>`), and the round's grammar results hold the review answers numbered after the deck's own (`nmax`). Adding a point to an existing deck: put it LAST, so the earlier parts and question numbers stay the same (4 ESO LP3 got -ed / -ing this way). **📚 Vocabulary too, and ONE grammar + ONE vocabulary game per LS (Roger, 16 Nov 2026: "And the same with the vocab, so now we only have one game of grammar and one game of vocab per LS"):** the points screen works for both kinds (`gDecks` = the course's decks of the game's kind; vocabulary points have `tab:"vocab"` or `"words"` and their decks `act`). The board's game list shows per LS only 🧩 Grammar · LSn and 📚 Vocabulary · LSn (`data-do="lsgame"`, `host.html?ls=<EL_LS id>&kind=grammar|vocab`, `H.lsg`), with the learning paths each group has played. In an LS game no deck is "this game" beforehand: `anchor(sel)` = the newest learning path of the LS with ticked points; it gets all its ticked questions and the game is saved as that deck (URL becomes `?deck=<anchor>&ls=&kind=` so a reload keeps it); every other ticked point gives 1, 2, 3 or all (`99`) 🔁 review questions. Nothing done yet → the LS's first learning path is ticked. The learning path pages and the Time plan still open a learning path's own game (that deck = this game). Code Breakers filters any kind with `tp` (only one game alone gets it), its review answers join that game's results. **🚀 Space rescue (Roger, 17 Nov 2026: "Space rescue for the grammar and vocabulary games", a third choice next to Hackers):** the Code Breakers round with another story: `lab/game/spacerescue.html` runs `cb-host.js` with `body.sr`; `live/{group}.cb.th = "sr"` (+ `cb.t0`, when the oxygen started) makes `cb-play.js` (and `play.js`'s `body.sr`) show it; the space rules and drawings are in `sr.js` (`window.ELSR`: planets by fuel, cargo pods ⛽/🛢️/🚀/☄️, rocket and planet SVGs, the journey strip) and the look in `sr.css`. No passwords, hacks or hacker avatars (no `cbAv` reads or writes); bits = fuel, `cb.rs` = planets reached (astronauts rescued, never lost). `host.js` `fmt()` = `quiz` | `cb` | `sr` (`el_game_fmt`); results are Code Breakers results with `th:"sr"` (results.html label, host.js ticks 🚀). New LS games need nothing more than their decks with `tab` points and `act` in `index.js`. **After the game (Roger, 17 Nov 2026: "after a game I should be able to go to class mode or end the class", "in a game students must be able to report problems"):** every board's end screen has 🎓 Class mode and 🏁 End the class (`lab/game/cm-after.js`: class mode still on → the students go back to it; off, e.g. its time ran out during the game → it starts again in the board's tab at the page it was on before the game (`live/{group}.go`, read by the board's own `taken()` reads), until the end of the class by `live/{group}.tt`; End the class = `go:null, fs:null`, `ce` = now, so `play.js` says the class is over). The player has ⚠️ in its top bar and "⚠️ I think my answer was right" on the Code Breakers / Space rescue ✗ screen: the same Problem window and save as the other pages (`el-report.js` `report` + `wakeFixer`), with the question on screen and the student's last answer (`cb-play.js` `where()`). **Students' Chromebooks (17 Nov 2026, two shut down in a Space rescue round):** nothing on the player (`body.play`) may animate a full-screen background (the drifting stars kept a 6×-slowed CPU 94 % busy; still stars: 1 %): no floating shapes in Code Breakers / Space rescue, a lighter confetti; the board keeps them. A ☄️ meteor is bad luck after a right answer: its own sound, never the wrong-answer buzzer.
- **🎯 What's in the exam · your notes (Roger, 9 Nov 2026, from a student suggestion: "a section covering what will be on the exam, as well as the key terms"; he chose one page with 📝 Your notes):** the LS home's `#notes` place (tile, board card and help all say "What's in the exam") shows, learning path by learning path in the LS page's order, ONLY what the student's group has done: the tabs in `ls/{id}.done[group]` (Time plan ticks and past class days, the board's "What we've done" map) and the class games it played (`ls/{id}.played`). Each learning path card has the tabs done with their day, 🔑 key terms (the `groups` word lists of the done tabs) and 🧩 grammar (the `explain` boxes of the done tabs), both at the student's route like the tab shows them (Pro: `data/native/<page>.js`), then that path's class games (theory, the student's notes, result, what to study), all folded so a Chromebook screen stays short. Learning paths dropped for the group are named as not in the exam; checkpoints, the sample exam, the final product and Reflect are left out; nothing not done shows. `shared/js/el-exam.js` reads each path's data file as text (no Firestore reads, `window.LP` untouched); `el-notes.js` draws it. The teacher's view adds 👁 What each group sees (pick a group and a route) to check that the exam asks only that. **A new LS:** add `<script src="../../shared/js/el-exam.js"></script>` before `el-notes.js` on its home page; learning paths need `groups`/`explain` in their panels for terms and grammar to show.
- **📓 My notebook + the whiteboard (Roger, 13 Nov 2026: "I need students to see this section as though it was hand written with an index … when I'm explaining theory I want students to only see a whiteboard … they can underline, highlight … this notes appear in their notes"; "a floating icon like a notebook always present"; "in the index … moving up and down the sections … add some sections"):** for students the `#notes` place is drawn by `shared/js/el-notebook.js` as a ring notebook that fills the whole screen (post-it tabs on top, ✕ Close; two pages on a Chromebook, one on a phone). Nothing scrolls ("no vull que facin scroll, sinó que passin pàgines"): the content is laid out page by page and what does not fit goes on to the next page; pages turn with ◀ ▶, arrow keys, a swipe or dragging a margin/corner, and 🖨 prints this page, chosen pages or all. Students edit it ("has de poder editar i afegir coses i moure seccions"): select words to highlight/underline/circle them (parts `m`), ✍ add a note after any part (parts `n`), ▲▼ move the parts of a section (parts `o.b`), ✏️ edit their whiteboard notes (saved back in their `gt-…` doc). Page 1 exam sticky note + legend, page 2 the index with page numbers and ▲▼ to reorder sections and ➕ Add a section, then per learning path a page (day in the margin, tabs done, key terms in handwriting, vocabulary games) and a 🧩 grammar page (explain boxes as glued slips, whiteboard notes, grammar games with score and "study again" notes). Own sections and the order: `students/{uid}/notes/nb-<LS>` parts `{o:{list, b}, n, m, s…:{topic, html, text, dr, day}}` (max 16 own sections; the notes rule allows ≤ 20 parts). While the teacher explains together (📘 Grammar together, 📖 Read together; `live/{group}.rt` before ⏱ Your turn) a student's page shows only `shared/js/el-whiteboard.js`: the projector's boxes/text with the current part marked + a handwriting page (`shared/js/el-pad.js`: pens, highlighters, underline, circle, box, draw, undo), saved in the `gt-…` notes doc (`-rd` for reading) with `html` (only `nb-*` classes, cleaned again when shown) and `dr` (drawings). **No copying the theory into the notes (Roger, 19 Nov 2026: "I don't want them to be able to copy the theory, since if they do, they have the information doubled in their notebooks"):** the notebook already shows every grammar box, so the whiteboard has no ⤵ copy button (📘 Grammar and 📖 Read together), a yellow slip above the page says notes are optional (own examples or the important things to remember), and 7 words in a row typed from the board bring up a tip that it is already in the notebook (typing is never blocked). Don't add a copy-the-theory button or "copy what you want" wording anywhere students take notes. `el-cloud.js` adds the floating 📓 button (bottom left) on every student page (LS2+ pages, and the board and other pages through `el_nb`, set by the board's 📓 My notebook card). Styles and the handwriting fonts (Caveat, Kalam): `shared/css/el-notebook.css`; "Aa Print letters" switches to plain letters. **A new LS:** also add `<script src="../../shared/js/el-notebook.js"></script>` before `el-notes.js` on its home page.
- **📖 Reading texts in the notebook + page flow (Roger, 25 Nov 2026; rule in REQUIREMENTS.md §3):** `el-exam.js` `readText(p, level, LP)` gives every part its `text`: `media.text` at the route the way the engines' `byLevel` picks it (Pro → Plus → Core, Starter → Base), or for 4 ESO LS2 Getting started (`media.text: true`) the chat in `media.chat` (= `readingChat`, one per route) and Pro's essay (`GS_NATIVE.readingHtml`, kept on `LP._gsn` by `lpData`); scripts and `on…` attributes are stripped. `el-notebook.js` draws it as `.nb-slip.nb-rtext` (plain print letters, no tilt; `el-notebook.css`) at the top of the step's part, so it shows only where the part shows (tab done in class, answered or with feedback). A new LS needs nothing: any panel with `media.text` gets it. Page flow (`flow`): the ✍ My notes from class label is `.nb-labw` (in `HEAD`, so it moves to the next page with the notes); `cleanAll` keeps a line per pasted `li`, `h1–h6`, `tr`, `blockquote`…; `.nb-notes div` / `.nb-mine div` split over pages (SPLIT is tested before the node is taken off the page, since those selectors need its parents), and a text run over 160 characters is placed sentence by sentence, so a long pasted note is never zoomed down to 35 %.
- **📓 Teacher feedback in the notebook (Roger, 18 Nov 2026: "apart from the corrections, students should see their feedback"):** each learning-path part of the notebook shows the teacher's answer to that step, `results/{activity}.replies[step]` and `answers[step].t` (the writing, Speaking and Talk corrections, as `el-corrections.js` shows them at the top of the tab; levels never): a green slip ✏️ Your teacher's correction / 💬 Your teacher's answer, and for a writing in `fbText` format the student's handed-in text (`state.steps[step].f[0].v`) with the changes marked, the mistakes with why and the corrected text (`feedback()` in `el-notebook.js`, `fb` on each part from `el-exam.js`). A step with feedback shows even if its tab wasn't ticked, is never "To do", and a learning path with feedback only still gets its chapter part; feedback on a step with no panel goes in one 💬 part at the end. No extra reads (the results are already loaded). Under each first-try answer the notebook writes the feedback line the step showed after ✓ Check (Roger, 18 Nov 2026: "All the activities done in class should have the feedback that appears in the normal menu"): `pageFb` in `el-exam.js` copies `ls2-engine.js` (typed: Correct! / Not yet. + `it.en`/`it.ca`; choice: + `it.ex`, else the `learningSupport.evidence` line, `it.h` for Starter/Base). Keep the two in step when the engine's feedback changes.
- **📓 Pages stay put (teacher report, 18 Nov 2026: "the index now is on another page", "a page distribution change when I zoom in and zoom out", lag typing in the ⚠️ window on iPad):** `el-notebook.js` fills each page `SLACK` (34 px) short of the paper's foot and the size watch (`check`) keeps the laid-out pages while the book has the same width and is at most SLACK shorter or `GROW` (160 px) taller; it never lays the pages out again while a text box (any input/textarea outside the notebook too) has focus or a `<dialog>` is open (the iPad keyboard), and checks again on focusout / dialog close. A real width change (browser zoom, rotation) still reflows. Don't go back to relaying out on every pixel.
- **📓 Notebook chapters + 📝 Exams (Roger, 13 Nov 2026: "Add a section (post-it) Exams, with the feedback and Final Product Feedback. Notebooks should have chapters call LS1 (Name) LS2 (Name)")**: each LS page's notebook is one chapter ("LS2 (Seasons & Celebrations)"), with chapter tabs under the book linking the other LSs of the course (`<ls>/index.html#notes`). The 📝 Exams section shows the exam corrected (results/{ls}/assessment: marks, questions to review, writing mistakes, what to review) and the final product mark + feedback (EL_GRADING[ls].fp.a), from the results el-cloud.js already keeps. Every LS home page (LS1 too) loads `lab/game/decks/index.js`, `decks/theory.js`, `el-criteria.js`, `el-grading.js` `el-fp-rubrics.js`, `el-criteria-easy.js`, `el-exam.js`, `el-notebook.js`, `el-notes.js` before el-cloud.js and has the `data-place="notes"` section with `#notesBox`. Games have no theory/notes any more (notes are taken in the SLP); old game notes sit inside the LP's Vocabulary/Grammar part and are editable (✏️, saved into that game's notes doc).
- **📓 Notebook cover, to-dos, full exam, FP rubric, quick notes (Roger, 13 Nov 2026):** page 1 (and every printout, unless 📘 With the cover is unticked) is a cover: favicon, English Lab, Roger Beltran · school year 2026/2027, the student's name, group and level ("B1 · Core"), and per LS the criteria worked in the notebook's activities with their degree (AE/AN/AS/NA, the teacher's final degree when there is one, else first tries) and indicator, the games' % right and the activities done well (≥ half right on the first try) n/m per competence. A tab done in class that the student hasn't done shows its questions unanswered under a "To do / Per fer" stamp, and the theory (key terms, How it works) of every tab shown. The exam shows every question of the report (right ones ✓ in blue, wrong ones corrected in green + why), the writing rubric rows and the feedback; the final product shows the rubric with the student's level underlined in every row (LS2: fp.rows + `ind`/`indCa` and results `levels[step][c]`; LS1: EL_FP_RUBRICS by the feedback parts). The floating 📓 (on LS1 too) always opens the notebook itself (problem report, 4ESOA, 16 Nov 2026: Roger "students should open their notebook whenever they press the notebook button"); it no longer opens the ✍ Quick note pad (`el-cloud.js` qnOpen, kept but unused): old quick notes were merged into `notes/nb-<LS>` parts.n.<id> = {sec: lp<n> or qn, html, text, dr, day, w: where}; notes whose section isn't in the notebook go to 📍 Quick notes. The red "View as" frame doesn't print.
- **🧪 Does it still open? (Roger, 24 Nov 2026: "okay go ahead", on a check so that a fix never breaks pages that worked):** `node tools/smoke/check.mjs [filter]` (Playwright, offline from the repo files; GitHub Action *Does it still open* on every pull request that changes more than `docs/` or `.md` files, about 2–3 minutes). It opens, at 1366×768 and 390×844 (phone emulation): `teacher.html` with the demo data of `tools/site-video/teacher-demo.mjs` (which now exports `D` and `body` and only serves or takes pictures when run itself) and clicks every `#tabs button[data-g]`: each must open with no error, be the only one lit (`.on` / `aria-current`), show a non-empty `#main` and not the same `#main` text as another item (a wrong link, as How it works opening Materials before #866); then `today.html` and its places (`#todo #homework #marks #progress #exam-practice #books #help`), every LS2 page except `exam.html` and the `*-model.html` videos, `lab/practice.html` and `lab/index.html`, as the invented students of `tools/site-video/board/demo.mjs` (4 ESO Core English, 2 ESO Base Catalan), each a fresh load. A page fails on an uncaught error (also one thrown in a timer, which the fixed test clock reports on the console), less than 40 characters of text, a phone page wider than 390 px (`scrollWidth`, not `innerWidth`, which grows when a phone zooms out), a repo file it asks for that is missing (except `MAY_BE_MISSING`: Practise probes LS1 data files that do not exist on purpose) or any request to Firestore, Firebase Auth/Storage or the school script (`REAL`). Every request outside `el.local` is blocked. `SHOTS=dir` saves a picture of each page. A new LS: add its folder to the student pages list (`S.C.dir`) when its demo exists; a new board place: add it to `BOARD`.
- **🙈 Students never see Towards Nativeness (Roger, 20 Nov 2026: "students see towards nativeness and that was a rule, they mustn't see it"):** Roger's own course (`4eso-towards-nativeness`, `personal:true` in `EL_LS`) is removed from `EL_LS` / `EL_CATALOG` by `el-catalog.js` for students, signed-out visitors and the teacher's 👁 student view (`elAsView()`: sessionStorage `el_as`, or `?student=` on the board's first load), so the student view shows exactly what students see. The teacher keeps it on the dashboard, in their own view and on the course's own pages; the tester keeps it. Checked on every PR by `tools/students-never-see/check.mjs` (GitHub Action "Students never see Towards Nativeness"); pictures with `tools/students-never-see/preview.mjs`.
- **🛟 Notebook layout safety net (Roger, 20 Nov 2026: "when I choose print the whole notebook there are pages missing"):** `layout()` in `shared/js/el-notebook.js` lays out each section in its own try/catch. A section that throws is kept WHOLE on one `.nb-page.nb-whole` (scrolls on screen, runs over more sheets on paper) and the layout goes on, so the Index always gets real page numbers ("00" in the Index = the layout stopped there). The error goes to the console and `window.elNbLayoutErrors`. Test harness idea: serve the repo with Playwright routes + `EL_DEMO_LS` (as tools/help-guide/capture.mjs) and `page.pdf()` (fires beforeprint/afterprint).
- **📓 in class mode (problem report, 2ESOB, 17 Nov 2026: "when we are listening to the teacher we can't take notes"; Roger, same day: "when students are in class mode, I want them to be able to go to their notebooks and do whatever they want"):** while 🎓 class mode keeps a student on a page, the floating 📓 button (and any link to an LS page's #notes) opens the whole notebook; class mode does not send them back while `#notes` is open, and a 🎓 Back to the class · <step> button bottom left (`cmNbBar`) takes them to the teacher's step. Closing the notebook sends them back as from any other page. A 🔒 pull-up (not class mode) still turns 📓 into the ✍ quick note pad (`qnOpen`).
- **✍ Notes while doing an activity (problem report, 2ESOB, 19 Nov 2026: "que quan estiguis fent una activitat puguis fer i posar notes a la teva llibreta"; Roger: "I want to do this"):** on every activity page of an LS from LS2 on (learning paths, Book, final product…; NOT LS1, the LS page, `exam.html`, `sample-exam.html`, recall checkpoints, nor a Recall step: those are "No notes") a signed-in student has a ✍ Notes button above 📓. It opens the handwriting page (`el-pad.js`) as a panel down the left side (the page moves over on ≥900 px; a bottom sheet on phones), the activity still usable. `el-cloud.js` `qnOpen({side:true})` saves it like the quick note into `notes/nb-<LS>` `parts.n.<id>` plus `p` (the tab) and `at: {b: "lp<n>-p-<tab>", e: 1}`, which `el-notebook.js` `inlinePut` places right AFTER that step's part (its own block, so a To do lock never covers it; no part yet = end of the LP section). Another tab with the panel open = that step's note; the same step's note comes back: ONE read of `notes/nb-<LS>` the first time the panel opens on a page, none otherwise. No copying the theory (#729): Ctrl+C/paste stay blocked (`el-nocopy.js`), a yellow slip says notes are their own examples / things to remember, and 7 words in a row of the step's theory boxes bring the "already in your notebook" tip (never blocks typing). The 📓 button itself still opens the notebook (16 Nov rule).
- Keep the existing course palettes: **2ESO green**, **4ESO blue**. Copy the reference page’s exact theme variables, tinted backgrounds, borders, shadows, typography and card treatment; do not approximate colours.
- Preserve responsive layouts, navigation, scoring, feedback and route-specific progress unless the requested change requires otherwise. Keep edits focused.

## Route language and scaffolding
- **Base reads English (Roger, 21 Nov 2026: "From now on base is also in English, no Catalan help here, the help in English"):** only Starter (`beginners`) and a student with no route get Catalan instructions, hints, help and feedback; Base (`insecure`) gets English like Core and Plus. This replaces every older line here that says "Starter and Base" or "Insecure" read Catalan. Code: `elCa(l)` in `el-catalog.js` is true only for `beginners`; every hard-coded check (`ls2-engine.js` `ca()`, `learning-path-support.js`, `section-next.js`, `el-todo.js`, `el-voice.js`, `el-mic.js`, `el-deck-points.js`, `el-level-notice.js`, `el-web-exam.js`, the print pages, `tools/marking/marks.mjs`, `tools/notify/*.mjs`, `tools/web-exam/writing.mjs`) follows it. Base keeps its **support**, in English: `sup()` in `ls2-engine.js` (Talk questions shown, slower voice, slow shadowing), Surprise questions shown at once with help words (`el-surprise.js`), and in the vocabulary games `CA(r)` (instruction language, Starter only) is apart from `CAM(r)` (the Catalan-meaning game content and options, Starter and Base). Each question's Catalan hint (`h:` in the data files, Starter) has an English twin (`he:`) that Base sees in the ✓ Check feedback (`learningSupport.evidence`, Roger, 21 Nov 2026: "Yes add the extra help in English"); a new LS writes both. **LS1 (My Real Routine) keeps Base in Catalan**: its pages are finished and their Base content is written in Catalan (`caLevel` / `snCa` keep that on LS1 pages). The exam's language follows the student's level, not the paper (`el-web-exam.js`): a Base student on the Base paper reads English.
- **✓ Check one question at a time (Roger, 16 Nov 2026: "I want students to be able to check each sentence of a set of sentences when doing an activity, since they end up checking the answers before finishing"):** in LS2 learning paths (`ls2-engine.js`, both courses) every question of a typed step (grammar, mixed, grammar in parts), the vocabulary practice and the reading / listening questions (`mc`) has its own small ✓ Check (`button[data-one]`; Enter in a box does the same); the step's button is now **Check all** and an empty question is never marked wrong. The mark stays the first try, question by question: `state.items[id_route]` (`per: 1`) gets each question at its first check, and `tries[k]` (`per: 1`, `full`) is the right first checks over all counted questions (not checked yet = 0, as an empty one was before). Done = every counted question answered and checked; a wrong answer counts as answered. **Last question typed (Roger, 22 Nov 2026: "It should have a tick saying done"):** when a ✓ Check leaves every counted question with an answer, the ones typed but not checked yet are checked with it, so the step ends with Step complete ✓; while the step is not done, the result names the questions still to answer or check ("To complete the step, answer and check question 9 (✓ Check)."; `LEFT` / `leftNote`). `state.ans[id].c` / `state.nb[id].c` keep which questions were checked; the notebook look freezes an activity only when every counted question is in it. **✏️ Correct it in the notebook (Roger, 19 Nov 2026: "I want them to be able to redo the questions one by one"):** in a frozen notebook look each counted question answered wrong has its own ✏️ Correct it, which opens only that question with its ✓ Check (Enter too), as often as needed; the correction is saved in `state.nb[id].re` (by question id: `{v, t, ok, at}`) and drawn over the first try, which shows crossed out under it, and the result adds "Your mark is still your first try: n / m". The mark (`state.items`, `state.tries`), 📓 My notebook (first try) and the laptop look (`state.ans`) never change (`nbFix` / `nbOpen` / `nbRedo` in the three `ls2-engine.js`; check: `node tools/answers-stay/nb-redo.mjs`). Records saved before (no `per` / `c`) never change. Not changed: the recall warm-up, secure checkpoints, the sample exam (`quiz`), book chapters and the micro-dictation. **4 ESO LS2 Getting started too (Roger, 24 Nov 2026, on a 4ESOA problem report: "I want them to check the questions one by one, too"):** this page has its own script, not the engine, so it got the same thing on its own (`ONE` / `oneAdd` / `oneGo` in `getting-started.html`): a ✓ Check on every question of Autumn words (Enter too), Reading and Listening, the step's button is **Check all**, questions checked alone keep their feedback after a reload (`state.one[step]`), and once every question has an answer the step gets its full check and Step complete ✓. Its recall warm-up keeps one Check.
- **Beginners (Roger, 26 Oct 2026):** a fourth route, A1, below Insecure, for students starting almost from zero. Everything Insecure gets in Catalan, plus heavier scaffolding: a short core word list per LS practised with spaced retrieval (flashcards and recall every session), readings of 60–100 words built from that list at 95–98 % known words with Catalan hover glosses, multiple choice or matching instead of open answers, the slow audio copy with English subtitles from the first viewing, sentence frames with gaps and a word bank, the same final product with fewer requirements, feedback as a Catalan hint and a second try, and a short "how did I do it?" box. Same listenings and videos as everyone, mixed pairs in class (not Beginners with Beginners). Marked on the same NA–AE scale against CEFR A1 can-do statements. **Only the teacher sets it**, like every route (students can ask for it from their board; the teacher accepts or declines). **In code:** `elCa(level)` (`el-catalog.js`) says whether a route reads Catalan; LS2 engines order routes `beginners < insecure < safe < daring`, an untagged item is for every route, and a text, list or rubric with no `beginners` version falls back to `insecure` (`byLevel`, `forLevel`, `final-rubric.js`, the marking PDF, the study PDF). Every LS2 Learning Path page has a Beginners route button (students only see their own). LS1 pages have no Beginners version: `applyLevel` shows them the Insecure one. New LS content adds `beginners` keys where the A1 version differs (readings, questions, tasks, rubric), designed in the LS project. **Seasons & Celebrations (4 ESO) and Free Time, Live! (2 ESO) have Beginners content on every page since 26 Oct 2026** (docs/4eso/ls2-seasons-and-celebrations/overview.md and docs/2eso/ls2-free-time-live/overview.md, section "Beginners route"): the convention is that untagged items are for every route and `r: 'insecure'` marks what starts at Insecure. **The programmes** (docs/4eso/programme.md and docs/2eso/programme.md) carry the Beginners route since 26 Oct 2026 (Roger approved): a Beginners product bullet, core word list and A1 grammar block per LS, and a final chapter "Resum de gramàtica i lèxic · ruta Beginners (A1)" that is the reference for designing Beginners content in any LS. **Starter really differs from Base (Roger, 31 Oct 2026, thread "Explain Starter vs Base learning paths"):** Starter must not be just Base minus the hardest questions. (1) In LS2 engine pages whose `LP` has `starter: true` (2 ESO LP2–LP6, 4 ESO LP3–LP6; never add it to a path students have started), Starter picks typed answers (recall, grammar, mixed, vocabulary practice) from three buttons: the answer plus two distractors (`d: [...]` on the item if given, else forms of the bracketed verb, else a set of confusable words, else other answers of the step); a wrong pick is crossed out and they try again with the Catalan hint. Starter also starts listenings and videos on the `-slow` copy (videos with English subtitles on) and gets 16 s per question in Talk. From LS3 on, every LP sets `starter: true`. (2) An item with `only: ['insecure']` is shown only to the routes listed (`forLevel` in the engines, `el-items.js`, `lp-worksheet.js`): use it for Base practice of a basic form that Core and Plus already know (the grammar-that-comes-back rule). (3) Every vocabulary panel gives Starter its own short `core.beginners` list from the programme's Starter lexicon, and no step should be identical for Starter and Base: Starter gets an easier task or support, Base at least one or two items or requirements more.
- **Native (Roger, 30 Oct 2026):** a fifth route above Daring for a native speaker preparing Cambridge C2 Proficiency (4 ESO only for now); students see it as **Pro**. English throughout. **Only the teacher sets it** (`ask:false` in `EL_LEVELS`: students cannot ask for it). Same LS topic and Learning Paths, but C2 Proficiency-style tasks: Reading and Use of English (Part 5 texts, open cloze, word formation, key word transformations, multiple-choice cloze), listening with attitude and implication questions on the class audio, writing to Part 1/Part 2 tasks (article, essay, proposal, letter; 240–320 words; AI feedback on the four Cambridge writing subscales). **Speaking: no recording** (Roger): each speaking step is a `plan` panel (`ls2-engine.js` `R.plan`): the question, a strategy, the student's written plan (first ideas, the best ones and why, the order), then a model plan; handed in as `state.steps[id]`. **In code:** `data/native/<page>.js` sets `window.LP.native = { panelId: panel }`, loaded after the page's data file; `usePanels()` swaps those panels in by id (same ids, so progress, checkpoints, locks and the dashboard work). Getting started (own renderer) reads `window.GS_NATIVE` from `data/native/getting-started.js` (recall, vocab, practice, reading, listening, speaking plan). Anything without a Native version (other panels, LS1, 2 ESO, texts and lists in `byLevel`/`forLevel`) falls back to Daring, including the model page and rubric fallback. **Extra Pro panels:** a `LP.native` entry whose id is not in the page and has `after: '<panel id>'` is inserted after that panel (e.g. the real-video listening: one real native-speed YouTube video per Learning Path on its topic, `realvideo`, with C2 attitude/inference questions written from the video's captions; Roger 30 Oct 2026: real videos only, no generated audio); it is not in `EL_ACTIONS`, so it never changes progress. Getting started shows `GS_NATIVE.video` under the listening. The final product rubric has its own `native` entry (video and email rows on the Cambridge scales, mapped to NA–AE). `el-route-names.js` relabels Native → Pro except in “Native American(s)”; never start a student-facing sentence with a capitalised “Native” otherwise. Question analysis ignores the Native route.
  - **Pro review rule (problem report, 2 Nov 2026):** a Pro panel must only refer to what the Pro student sees (no “the reading text” or “the chat” when Pro reads another text; name the tab and title instead), and Pro tasks are marked automatically (`mc`/`typed`), not free text to the teacher, except the final-product pieces and the speaking plans. Page-level instructions written for the other routes (e.g. Getting started reading) need a Pro version when the Pro content differs. Study cards: Pro has no Catalan, so the direction buttons stay hidden (`.dir-toggle[hidden]` in `layout.css`).
- **Language outside an LS page (site audit, 31 Oct 2026):** on course pages, English Lab and Practice there is no route on screen, so `el-cloud.js` uses the route of the course's latest LS (`homeLevel`, `elRouteOf`) for the Problem and Idea windows, the cards (announcements, problem answers, exam results, corrections) and the board link; the board keeps its own language (the LS on screen). The blocks on LS pages (not your group, not available yet, not open yet, first do the checkpoint) and their link back to the board ("Torna al meu tauler" / "Back to my board") follow the student's route too, Catalan with no route.
- **Insecure (and Beginners):** pedagogical instructions, explanations, strategy tips, checklists, step-by-step guidance and corrective feedback are in **Catalan**. Use clear scaffolding and worked examples; explain briefly what went wrong and what to notice.
- **Safe:** instructions and explanations are in **English**. Catalan may appear only as limited vocabulary support, such as hover/focus meanings.
- **Daring:** English-first, with greater learner autonomy and less scaffolding.
- **English remains the target language** for vocabulary, listening/reading input, model sentences, interview questions where appropriate, slide content and student production. Do not translate these into Catalan when adding scaffolding.
- Reuse route-aware support such as `shared/js/learning-path-support.js` where appropriate; verify language updates correctly when switching routes.

## Route names students see
- **Only the new names in the app (Roger, 30 Oct 2026: "no need for old names in the app any more"): Starter · Base · Core · Plus · Pro** for Beginners · Insecure · Safe · Daring · Native, on student pages and on the teacher dashboard (`EL_LEVELS[].name` is the new name). Code ids, data keys (`r:'insecure'`), docs and content sources keep the old names.
- **Students see levels, not support descriptions (Roger, 1 Nov 2026: "they might feel judged if they read molt suport… change it everywhere"):** under each route name a student-facing card shows only its CEFR label (`EL_CEFR` short label: 2 ESO A1 · A1+ · A2 · A2+; 4 ESO A1 · A2 · B1 · B1+ · C2), e.g. `<button class="route" data-level="beginners"><strong>Beginners</strong><span>A1</span></button>` on every LS page, the vocabulary games' route cards and the board's route request. Never write "molt suport", "strong support", "less support" and the like where students read it; `EL_LEVELS[].ca/.en` descriptions are for the teacher's dashboard only. LS1 too (Roger, 1 Nov 2026: "Yes, change LS1 too": only the card labels).
- `shared/js/el-route-names.js` relabels whole capitalised route words in visible text, titles, aria-labels and placeholders on every page, including re-renders and `teacher.html` (plans, tips, rubrics, marking PDF). `el-cloud.js` imports it; `teacher.html` loads it; a page without `el-cloud.js` adds `<script src="…/shared/js/el-route-names.js" defer></script>` next to `el-copyright.js`. Keep writing the old names in content; never capitalise "Safe", "Daring", "Insecure" or "Beginners" as ordinary English words in student content (write "safe" lowercase), or they get relabelled.

## Self-practice by level (English Lab)
- **`lab/practice.html` (Roger, 31 Oct 2026, thread "Build a self-practice area by level"):** word cards (turn over, or Catalan → English test), sentence builder, listenings with questions and shadowing, gathered from the student's LSs and organised by level. A level is a route with its CEFR label (`EL_CEFR`, `elCefr`, `elLevelLabel` in `el-catalog.js`): 2 ESO Starter A1 · Base A1+ · Core A2 · Plus A2+; 4 ESO Starter A1 · Base A2 · Core B1 · Plus B1+ · Pro C2 (from the table below). Students see and practise only their own level, with no level buttons (Roger, 31 Oct 2026: "those buttons where you can change the level should be only for the teacher's view"); the teacher and testers (not in "View as") choose any course and level. Students read their level as "2ESO Starter (A1)" on the board (`today.html`, plus a "Practise on your own" box and menu link), on the practice page and in the level notice card. **Students never see where the practice comes from** (Roger, 7 Nov 2026: "I don't want students to see where the info comes from. This is too much info"): no LS chips, no Learning Path list, no other-course sentence, no LP names on cards, listenings or clips; they got everything at their level in one list (since 25 Nov 2026 they choose the LS first: 🎯 Practise per LS below; still no learning path names). The teacher and testers keep the filters and source lines. **Only the tabs the learning path page shows** (Roger, 22 Nov 2026: "Why some 4ESO students could do the future forms if they are scheduled for next Wednesday?"): besides `elLpOpen` (a path planned whole for a later class stays out), `content()` drops for a student every tab of `elSecLockedFor(a, info, group, new Set())` (locked with the section switches, or on a later class day / no class day of the Time plan: the same test as the LP page), so 🎯 Practise never holds words, grammar, listenings or clips from a tab not opened in class yet; the teacher and testers still see everything.
- **🎯 Practise per LS (Roger, 25 Nov 2026: "I want practice per LSs, so you can choose on LS (grammar, vocab, listening, reading, writing, speaking) or both"; rule in docs/REQUIREMENTS.md › The student's pages):** `content()` now reads every LS of the course at the level once (`XA`, cached per `course|level`, every item tagged `ls` and `act`), and `view(XA, ls)` keeps one LS or `'all'` (plus the teacher's learning-path select `S.lp`), de-duplicating words per view. Screens: `chooser()` (no `S.ls`: one `.lsc` card per LS, newest first for students, with `counts()` per skill, plus `🔀 all`) → `skills()` (`← All LSs`, six tiles `TABS`: `words` `grammar` `listen` `read` `write` `speak`; no tile chosen = "Choose what you want to practise") → `body()`. The address holds the place: `#<ls id or all>/<skill>` (`fromHash`, `nav` pushes one history step per choice, an opened item pushes `{open}` state, `popstate` restores; old `#grammar` / `#build` / `#shadow` links open that skill with every LS mixed). New in `extract()`: `out.read` (an `mc`/`quiz` panel with `media.text`, shown by `listen()` with `QL()` = `X.read`), `out.write` (`writing` panels: `task`, `strict()` `frame` / `model` / `min` / `checklist` = the route's own value, no borrowing from other routes), `out.speak` (`speaking` / `talk`: `task`, `model`, `partner` at the route). `write()` lists 🧩 Sentence builder (`S.open='build'`, `build()`) and the tasks (textarea saved in `el_practice.write`); `speak()` lists 🎲 Random questions (`S.open='rq'`, `rq()`: `extract()` keeps the open tabs in `out.tabs` `{t: 'activity#panel', dir}`; `rqLoad` loads `shared/js/el-surprise.js` and each LS's `data/surprise.js` + `data/surprise-auto.js` once; `rqDraw` merges `elSurprise.pool({bank: ls id, tabs}, level)` of the LSs on screen and takes one per tier, written first, never last round's; no timer, recording starts with ▶ Start), the shadowing clips (`S.open='sh:<i>'`, `shadow(i)` draws one clip) and the tasks (`trecStart` records in the browser only). Grammar's `🎲 Mixed questions` (`S.open='mixq'`, `gFind`, `MIXQ`): 10 random items of the topics on screen, only the best score kept (`store.gram['mixq@<ls>@<level>']`). Listening and reading hints: `h` for Starter, `he` for the others (`qHint`).
- **🧠 Flashcards in Practise (Roger, 24 Nov 2026: "a flashcard activity with all the vocabulary and grammar done in each route and by LS… when we add new activities they appear there… spaced repetition… for the entire unit… like Anki… all mixed with statistics… and per LS… to study something specific"; decks hold "What the class has done so far"):** the wide 🧠 Flashcards tile on top of `lab/practice.html` (`#deck`). `extract()` adds `out.cards` from the same tabs Practise already shows (so `elSecLockedFor` keeps future and locked tabs out): one card per word of every panel with `groups` (Vocabulary steps, 4 ESO Getting started `words`; LS1 `vocabMap`) → `vCard` (front the English, back the Catalan or the Pro meaning + example, ⭐ core), and one per item of every `typed` panel whose id starts `grammar`, `mixed` or `vocabEx` at the route (`forLevel`) → `gCard` (front the sentence with its gap, back the sentence filled in with the answer marked and the rule `en` / Starter `ca`; Starter sees three options (`opts` or `choices()`), Base the authored `opts`). **gCard shows what is right (Roger, 25 Nov 2026: "the relative clauses flashcard are missleading"):** a question with `→` turns over to the part after the arrow filled in (its first letter capitalised for a full sentence; an answer that starts it capitalised; `i` → I) and that sentence is `say` (spaces before punctuation removed only for these new lines, so older recordings keep their keys); a `fix` with only `→ ___` uses the item's `fc` (the right sentence, answer marked); `fq` replaces the question on the card only (the open cloze line with its sentence); the card id stays `hash(it.q|a[0])`. `gAlts()` adds every other answer of `a` that only swaps who / whom / which / that ("who / that"), and the rule gets "who / that: both are right." when it doesn't say that. An answer word takes its capital from the question ("in november" → November). Part = the panel title, the part title (`parts[].title`) for a panel with several parts, "Mixed practice · <the path's grammar title>" for Mixed (never an LP name: students don't see where things come from). Card ids come from the text (`v:` + normalised word, `g:` + hash of question and answer), so a card keeps its progress across paths and a new question is a new card; a new SLP adds its cards with no extra work. `content()` makes `X.decks` (one per LS, newest first). `shared/js/el-flashdeck.js` (`ELFlashDeck.mount(host, {decks, ca, load, save, say})`, `summary`, `merge`, `next`) opens on **Choose an LS** (Roger, 24 Nov 2026: "it's not clear what they are selecting. I want the user to go to flashcards and there select LS or see statistics"): one big card per LS (cards to study today, new · learning · to review, % learnt, Open ›), 📊 My statistics (all LSs) and 🔀 Study all my LSs mixed; an LS opens its own page (← All LSs, 📊 Statistics, ▶ Study everything mixed, 🎯 Or study only one part), and closing a study session goes back to that page; the full-screen study (Space turns, keys 1–4 = Again / Hard / Good / Easy with the next gap on each button) and the stats (today, 🔥 days in a row, % remembered in 30 days of reviews, New / Learning / Young / Mature, the next 7 days, the last 5 weeks, each part). Scheduling: a simple Anki / SM-2 (learning steps 1 and 10 min, graduate 1 day, Easy 4 days; review Hard ×1.2, Good ×ease, Easy ×ease×1.3; ease 2.5, −0.2 on Again, −0.15 Hard, +0.15 Easy, min 1.3; Again on a review relearns at 10 min with half the gap; 15 new cards a day per LS; days start at 4:00). One part studies the whole part; a card not due yet keeps its date on Good / Easy. Progress: `el_flash_<uid>` in the browser and `students/{uid}/flashcards/all` `{p: JSON, at}` (rules: the student writes only that doc, teacher reads), read once when the decks open and merged (latest review of each card wins), saved 15 s after the last answer and on close / leaving the page. The teacher, testers seeing everything and the 👁 student view keep it in the browser only. No teacher view of it yet. The smoke check opens `lab/practice.html#deck`.
- **LS1 flashcard decks are cumulative (Roger, 25 Nov 2026: "this is wrong it cant all be make"):** `extract()` in lab/practice.html, `pg.kind === 'ls1'`, builds the groups like the LS1 page's `cumulativeGroups()` (insecure → the route's own key, same group name merged, no repeats), for the cards and for Practise's 🃏 Vocabulary alike; a route used to get only `vocabMap[route]`, so a Plus LS1 deck was only the MAKE / DO / HAVE / TAKE & PAY groups. Groups whose name is only verbs in capitals (`/^[A-Z]+(\s*&\s*[A-Z]+)*$/`) become one part, "Collocations: make, do, have, take & pay", so studying that part means choosing the verb. Card ids are the English text, so progress is kept; the routine words already have Kokoro audio (tools/voice/lines.json).
- **One teacher menu (Roger, 25 Nov 2026: "this menu should always be the same"):** `EL_TMENU` + `elTeacherMenu(o)` in el-catalog.js are the only list of the teacher's side menu. teacher.html `render0` draws it with `dash:true` (buttons with the old `data-do="tab"/"hplace"/"annnew"/"mtdlg"`, its badges passed in `extra`/`asks`/`counts`/`none`) and saves the numbers to localStorage `el_tmenu` (`elTmCache()`: waiting counts kept 12 h). Every other teacher page draws the same list as links (`.eltm`, el-app.css section 11): el-app.js `rail()` (wide list + `.eln` icon strip from `list:true`; mini strip on learning paths) and `side()` (Games / Practise / Flashcards `.sidebar`), el-ls-home.js `nav()` (`.nvs.eltm`); the brand says Teacher dashboard and links to teacher.html. Links: `teacher.html?g=<group or plan|corr|qa|cres|online|mat|how|ref|lss|none|tn>`, `?g=plan&p=week|msgs|tt`, `?do=annnew|mtdlg`, `?more=1` (phone More sheet); read in teacher.html's start IIFE (`CLS.link`, `CLS.place`, `CLS.todo`, run by `tmTodo()` in `firstDraw`). On a phone `elTeacherTabs()` draws the dashboard's tab bar (icons `EL_TPH_I`, shared with teacher.html `TPH_I`). Testers and the 👁 student view keep their menus. A new dashboard menu item goes in `EL_TMENU`, never only in teacher.html.
- **🧠 Flashcards apart from Practise (Roger, 25 Nov 2026: "I want practice and flashcards split in two. Students will see their flashcards under progress"):** `lab/practice.html#deck` is the Flashcards page (`FC` in practice.html: title 🧠 Flashcards, own lead, no Practise tabs or teacher filters, only the deck); `lab/practice.html` is Practise without the deck tab. Switching between the two reloads the page (`hashchange`). Menus: `elStudentMenu` (el-catalog.js) item `flashcards` under `progress` (keeps `?student=` in the student view), the teacher's el-app.js menu after Practise, the board's phone Me page (`today.html`, under My progress); on a phone the Flashcards page lights the Me tab and has no Practise / Games halves (el-app.js).
